UNCLASSIFIED
SUBJECT: Roster and Statistics of Japanese Educational
Institutions, April and November 1946.
The Acting Political Adviser has the honor to refer to
this Mission's despatch No. 202 of March 29, 1948, transmit-
ting copies of a special report of the Civil Information and
Education Section, General Headquarters, Supreme Commander for
the Allied Powers, entitled "Roster and Statistics of Japanese
Educational Institutions, April and November l946".
There are now enclosed two copies each of Parts 10 and 24,
of the above-mentioned report, covering respectively the pref-
ectures of Gumma and Miyagi. Future additions to this report
will be forwarded to the Department as they become available.

FROM:MG N
sUBJBCT: Attached.
USPOLAD forwards a copy of a resolu-
tion recently passed by the Diet confirming
the fact that the Imperial Rescript on
Education and other related rescripts are
null and void; that the Japanese people are
expeoted to make concentrated efforts to
recognize the ideals of the new educational
system as set forth in the Fundamental Law
on Education.
USPOLAD comments that Headquartere
welcomes the resolution which was warranted
by a tendency among the Japanese to retain
respect for the old rescripts.

=== Page 662 === (confidence 0.97)
DC
ACTION
THE FREIGN SERVICE
OF THE
is assigned to
UNITED STATES OF AMERICA
UNITED STATES POLITICAL ADVISER
FOR JAPAN
No. 389
RESTRICTED
PARTMÉNT OF STATE
Subject: Resolution Confirming Invalidity of Imperial Rescript on
Education.
894.42/6-2548
The Honorable
The Secretary of State,
Washington.
Sir:
I have the honor to enclose copy of a translation of a joint
1/
resolution (together with one copy of the original Japanese text)
adopted by the House of Councillors and the House of Representatives
of the Japanese Diet on June 19, 1948, confirming the invalidity of
the Imperial Rescript on Education and of other Imperial Rescripts
and Edicts. As the Department is aware, the Education Ministry is sued
instructions in November 1945 that the reading of the Rescript in
Schools and on ceremonial occasions was no longer compulsory. In May
M948, members of the education committees of the Diet charged that
these instructions were not obeyed in some s chools and other institu-
trons. During subsequent discussions in the Diet, it has been claimed
that the rescript runs counter to Article 98 of the new constitution
CID
which provides thst "no law, ordinance, imperial rescript, or other
action of Government, or part thereof, contrary to the provisions of
the Constitution, shall have legel force or validity".
DclA
The resolution adopted confirms that the Imperial Rescript on
Education, together with other Imperial Rescripts and Edicts as the
Imperial Rescript granted to the Men of the Army and Navy, the Imperial
CS/A
cla
Edict of 1908, and the Imperial Rescript granted to Youths and Students,
has been abolished and is null and void. The resolution further states
thet all copies of the rescripts and edicts will be recalled and that
all people are expected to concentrate on popularizing the ideals set
forth in the Basic Education Law.
C
There has been some discussion in Japan as to whether a néw
educational charter is needed, but the consensus appears to be that the
Constitution and the Basic Education Law already contain all the
894.42
essential precepts for education in a democracy. An editorial in the
2/
June 1, 1948 issue of the Asahi (five copies enclosed) points out that
issuance of another document would be superfluous and goes on to sey
thet it is more necessary to appreciate the spirit of the Constitution
and the Basic Education Law than to write a new charter on the subject.
We
RI STR ICTED
A 1 194
FILED

=== Page 663 === (confidence 0.99)
Tokyo's 389
June 25, 1948.
RESTRICTED
-2-
We believe that the adoption of the joint resolution was warranted
by the tendency of the Japanese people to retain respect for the reseripts
which were obstacles to the inculcation of democratic ideas. With wide
dissemination in Japan of the resolution, it is hoped that the Japanese
people will at long last become convinced of the invalidity of the
rescripts and look to the provisions of the Basic Education Law for
guidance in the education of their children.
Respectfully yours,
W. J. Sebald
Enclosures:
1.
Translation of a Joint Resolution
adopted by Diet on June 19, 1948
together with original Japanese text.
2.
Editorial from Asahi, June 1, 1948
(five copies)

=== Page 664 === (confidence 0.98)
Enclosure No. 1 to Despatch No. 589
dated June 25, 1948 from the Office
of the Political Adviser for Japan,
1
Tokyo, on the subject, "Resolution
Confirming Invalidity of Imperial
Rescript on Education".
(COPY)
RESOLUTION CONCERNING CONF IRMATION OF THE INVALIDITY
OF THE ILPERIAL R SCHIPT ON EDUCATION, etc.
In pursuence of the universal principle of mankind elucidated by
the Constitution of Japan, e have already legislated the Fundamental
Law of Education, in which we have solemnly declared the educational
ideal to foster a people eager to seek truth and peace, eradicating
completely and thoroughly the past errors in education centering upon
our State and our race.
The Imperial Rescript on Education, together with such other
Imperial Rescripts and Edicts as the Imperial Rescript granted to the
Men of the army and the Navy, the Imperiel Edict of 1908, and the
Imperial Rescript granted to the youths and Students, has been already
abolished and has become null and void.
However in consideration of mis-understandings that might still be
held by some people as if those Rescripts still preserve the same validity
as hitherto, we confirm the fact that they have already become null and
void, and we will cause the Government to recall all the copies of the
Imperial Rescript on Education and other Imperial Rescripts end Edicts.
We expect all the people to make concentrated efforts for through-
going populerization of the ideals of the new education indicated in the
Fundamental Law of Education so that the real authority of education may
be set up ana the national morality enhanced.
We are resolved as above.

=== Page 666 === (confidence 0.99)
NILFON TIMES:
June 2, 1948
WHL
PRESS COMMENTS
Tuesday, June 1
NEW EDUCATIONAL
CHARTER UNNECESSARY
ASAHI—The proposal made
by the education committees
of both Houses of the Diet to
scrap the Imperial Rescript on
Education is very welcome. It
is to be hoped thát some ap-
propriate steps will be taken
to inform the public thorough-
ly of the fact that the Rescript
has become completely null
and void.
After the copies of the Edu-
cational Rescript have been
withdrawn from all education-
al institutions and its invalida-
tion confirmed, there still re-
mains one problem, that is
whether a new educational
charter befitting a democratic
Japan is necessary or not.
Those in favor of a new chart-
er take thé stand that inas-
much as mere proclamation
of the invalidation of the Res-
cript falls short of eradicating
the deep-rooted influences it
has had in the minds of the
public; it. is appropriate to
ordain a new charter to re-
orient the educators and the
general public for dêmocratic
concepts of education.
What, then, should be con-
taiped in such a document?
We have the new Constitution
and the Basic Education Law.
The Constitution solemnly
ordains popular sovereignty,
renunciation of war, pious
hope for eternal peace and
banishment of tyranny and
slavery, oppression and in-
tolerance, whereas the,Basic
Education Law provides for
respect for the dignity of'the
individual, / aspirations to
truth and peace and education-
al principles for the creation
of worthy culture. What else
besides these precepts can the
new educational charter pro-
vide for? Issuance of another
document would only mean a
superfluous addition and add
to the perplexity on the part
of educators and the geneval
publie.
It is more necessary thor-
oughly to appreciate the spirit
of the Constitution and the
Basic Education Law than to
write a new charter on educa-
tion.
